[tool-dab] read_file ->   1347	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1348	    /* Snip the evacuating pools from list of pools we are retaining */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1349	    *last_pool_p = NULL;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1350	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1351	    /* Evacuate marked blocks from the evacuating pools into the
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1352	       avail pools. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1353	    while (cur_pool) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1354	      header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(cur_pool, sz_class);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1355	      header_t* end = POOL_END(cur_pool);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1356	      mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz_class];
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1357	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1358	      while (p + wh <= end) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1359	        header_t hd = (header_t)atomic_load_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1360	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1361	        if (!POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1362	          CAMLassert (!Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.MARKED));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1363	          CAMLassert (!Has_status_hd(hd, NOT_MARKABLE));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1364	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1365	          /* Reminder: since colours have rotated, UNMARKED indicates a MARKED
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1366	          (i.e live) block */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1367	          if (Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.UNMARKED)) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1368	            /* live block in an evacuating pool, so we evacuate it to
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1369	             * the first available block */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1370	            pool* to_pool = heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class];
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1371	            value* new_p = to_pool->next_obj;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1372	            CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(new_p));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1373	            /* if there are free blocks after this, use those */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1374	            mlsize_t wosize = Wosize_hp(new_p);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1375	            if( wosize > 0 ) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1376	              /* copy the header and free pointer over */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1377	              value* next = (value*)(new_p + wh);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1378	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1379	              CAMLassert(
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1380	                POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(to_pool, sz_class) <= (header_t*)next
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1381	              );
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1382	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1383	              CAMLassert((header_t*)next <= POOL_END(to_pool));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1384	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1385	              *next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(wosize - 1);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1386	              next[1] = new_p[1];
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1387	              to_pool->next_obj = next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1388	            } else {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1389	              value *next = (value*)new_p[1];
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1390	              to_pool->next_obj = next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1391	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1392	              if (!next) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1393	                /* This pool is full. Move it to unswept_full_pools */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1394	                heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class] = to_pool->next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1395	                to_pool->next = heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class];
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1396	                heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class] = to_pool;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1397	              }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1398	            }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1399	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1400	            /* Copy the block to the new location */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1401	            memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1402	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1403	            /* Set first field of p to a forwarding pointer */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1404	            Field(Val_hp(p), 0) = Val_hp(new_p);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1405	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1406	            /* Since there can be no blocks with the MARKED status, we use this
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1407	              to indicate that a block has been evacuated and any pointers to
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1408	              it should be updated. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1409	            *p = With_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.MARKED);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1410	          } else if (Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.GARBAGE)) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1411	            /* We are implicitly sweeping pools in the evacuation set and thus
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1412	               we must remember to call finalisers for Custom blocks that would
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1413	               have been swept in a subsequent major cycle. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1414	            if (Tag_hd (hd) == Custom_tag) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1415	              void (*final_fun)(value) = Custom_ops_val(Val_hp(p))->finalize;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1416	              if (final_fun) final_fun(Val_hp(p));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1417	            }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1418	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1419	            heap->stats.pool_live_blocks--;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1420	            heap->stats.pool_live_words -= Whsize_hd(hd);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1421	            heap->stats.pool_frag_words -= (wh - Whsize_hd(hd));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1422	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1423	            /* In the DEBUG runtime, we should overwrite the fields of swept
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1424	               blocks. Note: this pool can't be allocated in to again and so
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1425	               we overwrite the header and first fields too. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1426	            #ifdef DEBUG
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1427	            for (int w = 0 ; w < wh ; w++) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1428	              Field(p, w) = Debug_free_major;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1429	            }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1430	            #endif
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1431	          }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1432	        } else {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1433	          /* This tells us the number of spaces of size whsize after this */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1434	          mlsize_t wosize = Wosize_hd(hd);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1435	          p += wosize * wh;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1436	        }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1437	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1438	        p += wh;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1439	      }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1440	      /* move pool to evacuated pools list, continue to next pool */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1441	      pool *next = cur_pool->next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1442	      cur_pool->next = evacuated_pools;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1443	      evacuated_pools = cur_pool;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1444	      cur_pool = next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1445	    }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1446	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1447	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1448	  CAML_EV_END(EV_COMPACT_EVACUATE);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1449	  caml_global_barrier(participating_count);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1450	  CAML_EV_BEGIN(EV_COMPACT_FORWARD);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1451	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1452	  /* Second phase: at this point all live blocks in evacuated pools
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1453	    have been moved and their old locations' first fields now point to
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1454	    their new locations. We now go through all pools again (including
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1455	    full ones this time) and for each field we check if the block the
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1456	    field points to has the header status MARKED - if it does then the block
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1457	    has been evacuated and we need to update the field to point to the new
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1458	    location. We do this by using the forwarding pointer that is in the first
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1459	    field of the evacuated block. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1460	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1461	  /* First we do roots (locals and finalisers) */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1462	  caml_do_roots(&compact_update_value, 0, NULL, Caml_state, 1);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1463	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1464	  /* Memprof roots and "weak" pointers to tracked blocks */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1465	  caml_memprof_scan_roots(&compact_update_value, 0, NULL,
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1466	                          Caml_state, true);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1467	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1468	  /* Next, one domain does the global roots */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1469	  if (participants[0] == Caml_state) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1470	    caml_scan_global_roots(&compact_update_value, NULL);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1471	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1472	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1473	  /* Shared heap pools. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1474	  for (int sz_class = 1; sz_class < NUM_SIZECLASSES; sz_class++) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1475	    compact_update_pools(heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class]);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1476	    compact_update_pools(heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class]);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1477	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1478	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1479	  /* Large allocations */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1480	  for (large_alloc *la = heap->unswept_large; la != NULL; la = la->next) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1481	    header_t* p = (header_t*)((char*)la + LARGE_ALLOC_HEADER_SZ);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1482	    if (Has_status_val(Val_hp(p), caml_global_heap_state.UNMARKED)) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1483	      compact_update_block(p);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1484	    }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1485	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1486	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1487	  /* Ephemerons */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1488	  struct caml_ephe_info* ephe_info = Caml_state->ephe_info;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1489	  compact_update_ephe_list(&ephe_info->todo);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1490	  compact_update_ephe_list(&ephe_info->live);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1491	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1492	  CAML_EV_END(EV_COMPACT_FORWARD);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1493	  caml_global_barrier(participating_count);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1494	  CAML_EV_BEGIN(EV_COMPACT_RELEASE);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1495	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1496	  /* Third phase: free all evacuated pools and release the mappings back to
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1497	      the OS.
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1498	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1499	      Note that we may have no "available" pools left, if all
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1500	      remaining pools have been filled up by evacuated blocks. */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1501	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1502	  pool* cur_pool = evacuated_pools;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1503	  while (cur_pool) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1504	    pool* next_pool = cur_pool->next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1505	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1506	    #ifdef DEBUG
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1507	    for (header_t *p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(cur_pool, cur_pool->sz);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1508	         p < POOL_END(cur_pool); p++) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1509	      *p = Debug_free_major;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1510	    }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1511	    #endif
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1512	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1513	    pool_free(heap, cur_pool, cur_pool->sz);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1514	    cur_pool = next_pool;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1515	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1516	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1517	  CAML_EV_END(EV_COMPACT_RELEASE);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1518	  caml_global_barrier(participating_count);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1519	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1520	  /* Fourth phase: one domain also needs to release the free list */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1521	  if( participants[0] == Caml_state ) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1522	    pool* cur_pool;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1523	    pool* next_pool;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1524	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1525	    caml_plat_lock_blocking(&pool_freelist.lock);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1526	    cur_pool = pool_freelist.free;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1527	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1528	    while( cur_pool ) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1529	      next_pool = cur_pool->next;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1530	      /* No stats to update so just unmap */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1531	      caml_mem_unmap(cur_pool, Bsize_wsize(POOL_WSIZE));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1532	      cur_pool = next_pool;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1533	    }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1534	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1535	    pool_freelist.free = NULL;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1536	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1537	    caml_plat_unlock(&pool_freelist.lock);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1538	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1539	    /* We are done, increment our compaction count */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1540	    atomic_fetch_add(&caml_compactions_count, 1);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1541	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1542	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1543	  caml_gc_log("Compacting heap complete");
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1544	  CAML_EV_END(EV_COMPACT);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1545	}
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1546	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1547	/* Compaction end */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1548	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1549	struct mem_stats {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1550	  /* unit is words */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1551	  uintnat allocated;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1552	  uintnat live;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1553	  uintnat free;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1554	  uintnat overhead;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1555	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1556	  uintnat live_blocks;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1557	};
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1558	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1559	static void verify_pool(pool* a, sizeclass sz, struct mem_stats* s) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1560	  for (value *v = a->next_obj; v; v = (value*)v[1]) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1561	    CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(v));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1562	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1563	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1564	  {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1565	    header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(a, sz);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1566	    header_t* end = POOL_END(a);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1567	    mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz];
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1568	    s->overhead += POOL_SLAB_WOFFSET(sz);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1569	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1570	    while (p + wh <= end) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1571	      /* This header can be read here and concurrently marked by the GC, but
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1572	         this is fine: marking can only turn UNMARKED objects into MARKED or
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1573	         NOT_MARKABLE, which is of no consequence for this verification
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1574	         (namely, that there is no garbage left). */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1575	      header_t hd = Hd_hp(p);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1576	      CAMLassert(
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1577	        POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd) ||
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1578	          !Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.GARBAGE)
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1579	      );
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1580	      if (!POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1581	        s->live += Whsize_hd(hd);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1582	        s->overhead += wh - Whsize_hd(hd);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1583	        s->live_blocks++;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1584	      } else {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1585	        /* count the free block and any that follow it (stored in the
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1586	           size bits in the header)*/
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1587	        s->free += wh * (1 + Wosize_hd(hd));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1588	        p += Wosize_hd(hd) * wh;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1589	      }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1590	      p += wh;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1591	    }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1592	    CAMLassert(end == p);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1593	    s->allocated += POOL_WSIZE;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1594	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1595	}
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1596	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1597	static void verify_large(large_alloc* a, struct mem_stats* s) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1598	  for (; a; a = a->next) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1599	    header_t hd = *(header_t*)((char*)a + LARGE_ALLOC_HEADER_SZ);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1600	    CAMLassert (!Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.GARBAGE));
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1601	    s->allocated += Wsize_bsize(LARGE_ALLOC_HEADER_SZ) + Whsize_hd(hd);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1602	    s->overhead += Wsize_bsize(LARGE_ALLOC_HEADER_SZ);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1603	    s->live_blocks++;
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1604	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1605	}
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1606	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1607	static void verify_swept (struct caml_heap_state* local) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1608	  struct mem_stats pool_stats = {0,}, large_stats = {0,};
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1609	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1610	  /* sweeping should be done by this point */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1611	  CAMLassert(local->next_to_sweep == NUM_SIZECLASSES);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1612	  for (int i = 0; i < NUM_SIZECLASSES; i++) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1613	    CAMLassert(local->unswept_avail_pools[i] == NULL);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1614	    CAMLassert(local->unswept_full_pools[i] == NULL);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1615	    for (pool *p = local->avail_pools[i]; p; p = p->next)
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1616	      verify_pool(p, i, &pool_stats);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1617	    for (pool *p = local->full_pools[i]; p; p = p->next) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1618	      CAMLassert(p->next_obj == NULL);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1619	      verify_pool(p, i, &pool_stats);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1620	    }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1621	  }
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1622	  caml_gc_log("Pooled memory: %" CAML_PRIuNAT " allocated, "
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1623	              "%" CAML_PRIuNAT " free, %" CAML_PRIuNAT " fragmentation",
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1624	              pool_stats.allocated, pool_stats.free, pool_stats.overhead);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1625	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1626	  verify_large(local->swept_large, &large_stats);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1627	  CAMLassert(local->unswept_large == NULL);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1628	  caml_gc_log("Large memory: %" CAML_PRIuNAT " allocated, "
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1629	              "%" CAML_PRIuNAT " free, %" CAML_PRIuNAT " fragmentation",
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1630	              large_stats.allocated, large_stats.free, large_stats.overhead);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1631	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1632	  /* Check stats are being computed correctly */
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1633	  CAMLassert(local->stats.pool_words == pool_stats.allocated);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1634	  CAMLassert(local->stats.pool_live_words == pool_stats.live);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1635	  CAMLassert(local->stats.pool_live_blocks == pool_stats.live_blocks);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1636	  CAMLassert(local->stats.pool_frag_words == pool_stats.overhead);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1637	  CAMLassert(local->stats.pool_words -
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1638	         (local->stats.pool_live_words + local->stats.pool_frag_words)
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1639	         == pool_stats.free);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1640	  CAMLassert(local->stats.large_words == large_stats.allocated);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1641	  CAMLassert(local->stats.large_blocks == large_stats.live_blocks);
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1642	}
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1643	
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1644	void caml_cycle_heap_from_stw_single (void) {
[tool-dab] read_file ->   1645	  struct global_heap_state oldg = caml_global_heap_state;

Let's check header mlvalues/memory definitions for header fields, Make_header, Wosize_hd etc. The run length is stored in "wosize" of free header flags. But note: free headers use Make_header(wosize, No_scan_tag, NOT_MARKABLE). The wosize here is number of contiguous free blocks *following* this onevoice.

Potential bug: In `pool_sweep`, when merging, after updating last_free_block run length, it doesn't update `last_free_block` pointer? That's fine, remains same block as run start extends. It then does `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);` skipping the free blocks following p alias hd's wosize. Then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` to move to next non-free block after run.

But there may be a subtle issue: When current free block hd is merged with previous free block, the code doesn't update last_free_block to p; it extends previous. It then uses `Wosize_hd(hd)` to skip following contiguous free blocks. If hd was a POOL_FREE_HEADER(0) (newly swept garbage), Wosize_hd(hd)=0 so p += wh*0 = p, then p += Whsize_hd(hd) = p+wh. Good.

Another potential bug: `Wosize_hd` vs `Whsize_hd` for free header. In pool_initialize: `p[0] = POOL_FREE_HEADER(pool_blocks-1);` sets wosize = pool_blocks-1 (number of contiguous following blocks). Good.

In `pool_allocate`, if allocating first block from a run of length N (meaning N following free blocks), header wosize N. After splitting, at p+wh, sets wosize N-1. But wait: if N=0, no following, next = p[1]. If N>0, there are following contiguous free blocks mainly representing the rest of the initial run Bradley. But this is actually wrong in one scenario: Suppose run consists of blocks B0, B1, B2, ... contiguous, header at B0 has wosize = total-1, and B0[1] points to next free block after the run (possibly 0). Allocate one block from B0. The new free list head should point to B1 (because B0 is now the allocated block). B1 should be a free block with wosize total-2 (blocks following B1 within the run), and B1[1] should be B0[1]. The code does exactly: `*next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1); next[1] = p[1];`. So B1[1] = old pointer. Good.

But hold on: What about p[1] pointer of the allocated block B0? It becomes garbage / overwritten by actual allocation, fine.

Potential bug at line 464 in pool_allocate:

This asserts p[1] is 0 or free. But in the run representation, p[1] stores pointer to next free block beyond the entire run; it should be free head or 0. OK.

Let's consider actual crash while bootstrapping. Could be due to `pool_sweep` merging logic incorrectly updating `last_free_block` when merging, specifically when `last_free_block` is not updated and then later at end `last_free_block[1] = 0`. Fine.

But there might be bug with `pool_sweep` `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` when hd is the newly-created free header with wosize maybe large after merging. Wait, if we merge current block into last_free_block, we extend last_free_block's run length, but we still use hd's Wosize to skip over the blocks following hd. That's correct because those blocks were part of hd's own run and are now included in last_free_block. The skip `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` skips over the blocks that were following hd, and then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` advances past hd itself. Total skip = (Wosize_hd(hd)+1)*wh, the entire run starting at p. Good.

But what if hd has been merged into last_free_block but hd's own Wosize does NOT reflect the contiguous free blocks that were already part of last_free_block? Let's think: last_free_block's run extends up to p, meaning previous free run ends right before pament. hd is a free block p, with its own following free blocks (if any). When merged, previous run now extends to include hd and hd's following run. We need to skip hd's following blocks, which Wosize_hd(hd) counts. Good.

Now consider when current block p is a free block that is *within* a previously established run but the sweep reaches it? Wait sweep iterates block by block, and when it hits a free block header, it skips its run. It never iterates the blocks inside a run individually.Ordermoment. So okay.

Let's search for TODO or suspicious comments. The user says "I have been making some changes to the OCaml garbage collector, improving the sweeping performance by run-length compressing the free space in the major heap. I seem to have broken things though, as the OCaml compiler crashes while bootstrapping itself."

The specific feature: run-length compressing free space in major heap. The implementation in shared_heap.c appears to be the change. There might be a bug introduced by the user in this code. We need to find and fix.

Let's examine `pool_sweep` more carefully; potential out-of-bounds? The loop condition `while (p + wh <= end)` then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` for free with skip. Suppose a free block at the last block of pool with Wosize_hd(hd) = 0 and p + wh == end: then after processing p, `p += wh * 0; p += wh`, so p becomes end, loop ends. Good.

Suppose at p near end with a free run extending to end: e.g., free header at p with wosize = 1 (blocks p, p+wh, p+2wh? Wait wosize counts following blocks). If p + (wosize+1)*wh == end. The loop processes p: skip `p += wh*wosize` then `p += wh` -> end. Good.

Now potential issue in `pool_sweep` early termination when `p+wh <= end` but there might be a block whose header spans beyond wh (large object in pool)? But all blocks in a pool are smaller than wh, with Whsize_hd(hd) <= wh, as asserted. The loop uses `Whsize_hd(hd)` to step, so for live blocks it skips based on header size, not necessarily wh. Wait! Important: In a pool of sizeclass sz, each block has wosize at most the maximum for that sizeclass, and actual `Whsize_hd(hd)` could be less than wh correspond to actual object size. The sweep loop works: for live blocks, `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` steps to next block, not necessarily exactly wh. But free block headers represent a run of *pool blocks* of size wh; each block slot in pool is wh in size (even if the object's used wosize smaller, there is fragmentation). Let's re-examine.

The pool layout: each sizeclass has blocks of uniform size wh (the sizeclass's block size). Objects allocate from these blocks, using a header with actual wosize <= max for sizeclass ACA. Fragmentation per block is wh - Whsize_hd(hd) (wasted inside the block). So when traversing allocated blocks, p should advance by the full block size wh, not by the actual object whsize? Wait, then what does `Whsize_hd(hd)` mean for stepping? Let's examine original OCaml 5 shared_heap.c upstream (if I recall). Actually in OCaml 5, pools consist of "slots" of size `wsize_sizeclass[sz]`; each object allocated occupies `whsize` words (<= wh) and the slot has that much. But p advances by `Whsize_hd(hd)` for live blocks? Hmm.

Let's look at `pool_initialize`: `POOL_FIRST_BLOCK` = pool + POOL_SLAB_WOFFSET(sz); pool_blocks = (end - p) / wh. It sets p[0] = POOL_FREE_HEADER(pool_blocks-1). That treats pool as sequence of wh-sized blocks. When an object is allocated, in `caml_shared_try_alloc`, `p = pool_allocate(local, sz)` returns a block start (slot), then header is `Make_header(wosize, tag, colour, reserved)` where wosize is actual object size, not necessarily wh. If allocated object smaller than wh, scanning must advance by the actual header size or by wh? In pools, blocks are all same size wh, and objects don't extend beyond their slot may use less than full slot. To find next object, you need to advance by wh, not by actual object size, because the slot boundary is fixed. But in the GC sweep/scan code, they use `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` to go to the next slot? That would only advance by actual object size and overlap next block if object smaller? Unless `Whsize_hd(hd)` for a block allocated in a pool is actually the sizeclass block size, i.e., the header's wosize is padded to wh? Each sizeclass has a maximum size wh. Objects of varying sizes up to wh fit in slots. The pool is divided into slots of size wh. So iteration through pool should step by wh for every slot, and object headers are stored at slot start.

```
  header_t hd = *p;
  ...

```

`Whsize_wosize(sz) = sz+1`. So `Whsize_hd(hd)` for a free header with header wosize = k (number of following free slots) = k+1. In pool_sweep, after a free block/run, it does:
- `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` -> skips the k following slots.
- `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` -> adds k+1 (!!) more words, not wh.

That's definitely wrong. For a free run with k following blocks, after `p += wh*k` (skip following slots), we are at the block after the run? Wait let's compute:
- At start, p points to first free slot of run (at index i).
- Run occupies slots i..i+k (total k+1 slots).
- Next slot after run is at index i+k+1, address p + (k+1)*wh.
- The code does `p += wh * k` (skips k slots, lands on slot i+k, the last slot of run). Then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` = p + (k+1) words. For k>0, this overshoots massively (unless wh=1). For k=0, `p += wh*0 + Whsize_hd(0)= p + 1` which is also wrong unless wh=1? Wait but for k=0, run is one slot; next slot should be p+wh. But the code adds Whsize_hd(hd) = 1, not wh, so it would land inside the same slot (just after header), unless wh==1 corrections.

Hold on, are headers stored differently? The header is at p, and `Whsize_hd(hd)` is the total header size in *words* including header. For a live object, Whsize_hd(hd) = wosize+1, which is the actual object size including header, and stepping by that is correct if objects are packed, not fixed slots. But pools... Hmm.

Let's re-examine the pool design. Maybe pools do NOT use fixed-size slots! In OCaml 5's shared heap, each pool is divided into "slabs" of fixed maximum size wh, and the pool_sweep original may indeed step by `Whsize_hd(hd)` for live objects because object headers store actual wosize, and when an object is allocated in a slab of larger max, the remaining slack is not part of the object. Wait, but then how does the allocator know the next slot? Let's look at `pool_allocate`: it returns p = r->next_obj (a slot). It never packs multiple objects into one slot. So the next slot after a live block must be at p + wh, not p + actual object size. Therefore stepping by actual size would be wrong.

But maybe the free-list is not represented by slot boundaries; it may represent *contiguous free words* where a "block" of size wh is a free block. In `pool_initialize`, the whole pool is one free run of pool_blocks slots. Each slot is wh. So yes, step must be wh.

Let's verify with `caml_shared_try_alloc` stats: s->pool_live_words += whsize; s->pool_frag_words += wh - whsize; so each live object occupies one slot of wh words, with frag = wh - whsize. This confirms slots are fixed wh. So sweep and scan must advance by wh across slots, regardless of object header size.

So line 650 `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` is likely the introduced bug. But maybe original code also had `p += wh;` and the user erroneously changed it when implementing run-length compression to account for hd size? Let's look at the surrounding code to see if there's a variable naming oddity. Actually, if the original code was:

```
p += Wosize_hd(hd) * whDOIsomething
...
p += wh;
```

Then run-length optimization might have modified the first skip. Let's look at `pool_finalise`, `calc_pool_stats`, `verify_pool`, `compact_update_pools` which all use `p += wh` after either live or free run (after `p += wh*Wosize_hd(hd)`). So line 650 is inconsistent. The user likely changed the final increment from `p += wh` to `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` accidentally, or maybe intended to handle something but broke it. Let's verify by computing a simple scenario.

Consider a pool for sizeclass wh=8, with 100 free slots. pool_initialize: p[0] = free header wosize=99, p[1]=0. pool_sweep:
- hd = free wosize 99.
- p += wh*99 = p + 792 (skips 99 slots)
- p += Whsize_hd(hd) = p + 100 words (instead of 8) => overshoots beyond pool end by ~92 slots. Then loop condition `p + wh <= end` likely false, loop ends, `p` not equal end but CAMLassert(p == end) at line 652! That would crash immediately when sweeping an all-free pool. In fact `CAMLassert(p == end)` would fail. Unless asserts disabled in release runtime. In debug build, crash. In production build, maybe memory corruption and later crash.

But wait, is `pool_sweep` called on a newly initialized pool in unswept_avail_pools? At cycle start, avail pools are moved to unswept; newly initialized pools created during previous cycle are in avail and may have next_obj pointing to all-free run. Then sweep on unswept_avail_pools would process them. Even before that, pool_find calls pool_sweep on unswept_avail_pools to find a pool—so the first pool created would be swept maybe multiple times? Let's trace.

At allocation of a new pool in `pool_find`, `pool_initialize` sets `local->avail_pools[sz] = r` with r->next_obj pointing to first free slot)Skip. When GC cycle happens, `caml_cycle_heap` moves avail_pools to unswept_avail_pools, and calls `caml_adopt_all_orphan_heaps`, etc. Then sweeping starts (caml_sweep) and eventually pool_sweep on unswept_avail_pools. Pool is all free. With the bug, `p` overshoots, assertion fails or memory corruption Douglas. This would indeed crash.

But is line 650 `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` maybe intentional for a different representation where free header's wosize encodes total run length not following count? Let's analyze representation from comments: "The wosize is used to indicate the number of contiguous free blocks that follow." So a free header at p with wosize k indicates k following free blocks. So slot advancement after processing free run should be `p += (k+1)*wh`, i.e., `p += wh * (Wosize_hd(hd) + 1)`. The code currently does `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd); ... p += Whsize_hd(hd)`. For this to equal `(k+1)*wh`, we'd need `Whsize_hd(hd) = wh`, which is false except wh=1. So bug is clear.

Wait, but for live blocks, the code does `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` which might also be wrong for slot-based iteration. Let's check what upstream original uses. Since this is the run-length compression change, maybe original code was:

```
if free:
  p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd); // skip contiguous free blocks
p += wh;
```

and the user changed `p += wh` to `p += Whsize_hd(hd)`. But for live blocks, that's also wrong because object size not equal slot size. Unless... Let's check `pool_allocate`: when a slot is allocated, the header is written with wosize = requested size. When sweeping, a live slot could have actual wosize less than wh, so `Whsize_hd(hd) < wh` and `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` would point into the same slot, not the next slot. That would definitely break.

But wait, maybe in a pool, the pool_sweep iterates not by "slot" but by actual object size, and the free-list "blocks" are not slot-sized but actual contiguous word ranges? Let's reconsider.

Look at `POOL_FIRST_BLOCK` and `POOL_END`: pool size POOL_WSIZE = 4096 words. `POOL_SLAB_WOFFSET(sz) = POOL_HEADER_WSIZE + wastage_sizeclass[sz]`. So first block is after header + wastage. `pool_blocks = (end - p) / wh`. So the pool contains `pool_blocks` slots of wh words each. This strongly suggests fixed-size slots.

In `caml_shared_try_alloc`, when allocating object of whsize <= SIZECLASS_MAX, it uses sizeclass for that whsize custody, allocates one slot of size wh (>= whsize), and sets header with actual wosize. The slack `wh - whsize` is "fragmentation". So the object does not extend into the next slot. Therefore traversal must step by wh per slot, regardless of object header wosize.

Thus `pool_sweep` should step by wh for live blocks too. The line `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` at 650 looks wrong for both live and free. But wait, does the code handle the object header's actual size to account for "block" being the actual object size? Let's read `calc_pool_stats` again:

```
while (p + wh <= end) {
    hd = ...
    if (!free) {
      s->pool_live_words += Whsize_hd(hd);
      s->pool_frag_words += wh - Whsize_hd(hd);
      ...
    } else {
      p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd); /* skip contiguous free blocks */
    }
    p += wh;
}
```

Yes: for live, it adds frag wh - Whsize_hd(hd) (implying actual object uses Whsize_hd(hd) within slot of wh), then advances by wh. So pool_sweep must also advance by wh after live. The current line 650 advances by Whsize_hd(hd), which would cause p to not align with slots Justin.

Therefore the bug is likely line 650: should be `p += wh;`, not `p += Whsize_hd(hd);`. But then what about the free-run skip? The sequence:
```
p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);   // If hd was free, skip the following free slots
p += wh;                   // move to next slot after the run
```
For a free header with Wosize = k, p advances by (k+1)*wh, landing at next slot after the run. Correct.

For a live header, p advances by wh, landing at next slot. Correct.

Let's verify with existing upstream code snippets in other functions:
- `pool_finalise` line 787-789: `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);` then `p += wh;`
- `calc_pool_stats` lines 301-304: same.
- `compact_update_pools` lines 1124-1126: same.
- verify_pool lines 1588-1590: same.

The only odd one is pool_sweep. So yes, likely user accidentally left `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` (maybe from old code before slot change) in pool_sweep. This is a classic subtle bug.

Let's confirm by looking at whether `pool_sweep` maybe uses `Whsize_hd(hd)` elsewhere historically. Could grep shows line 650 only. So fix is change `p += Whsize_hd(hd);` to `p += wh;`.

But wait—should we also check whether there are other introduced bugs? The task says "I have been making some changes... run-length compressing the free space in the major heap". The bug could be multiple. Let's examine the run-length compression logic beyond line 650andreview all modifications for correctness confident. Let's search for comments and compare with known upstream implementation to see if any other consumer wasn't updatedholiday.

Let's examine `pool_sweep` merging logic for another subtle bug: after merging a newly freed block into the previous run, the code uses `*last_free_block = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(last_free_block) + Wosize_hd(hd) + 1)`. Here `Wosize_hd(hd)` is the number of following free blocks (initial run length). Suppose last run has length L (following count = L-1), current p is free with following count M. Contiguity condition checks previous run ends exactly at p. After merging, new run length = L + (M+1) blocks, so new following count = L+M. Code: Wosize_hp(last_free_block) + Wosize_hd(hd) + 1 = (L-1) + M + 1 = L+M. Correct.

Now, `last_free_block` remains pointing to previous start, and we skip current run. Good.

Potential issue: When current hd is free with Wosize = k and it is *not* merging (there's a live block between previous and current), the code sets `last_free_block[1] = p`, and sets `last_free_block = p`. But it does not set anything about p's header or p[1]—they're already correct from previous sweep or allocation. Good.

Now consider `all_used` detection: starts 1; set 0 when garbage converted or free block encountered. If pool all live (no free), all_used remains 1able, a->next_obj stays 0 (set at start), and pool moved to full list (because release_to_global_pool cannot be true if any live block—release flag set 0 at line 648). Wait `release_to_global_pool` is a parameter; if pool has any live block, set 0. `all_used` true only if no free blocks. But if the pool was previously a full pool and all blocks remain live, `all_used=1`, pool moved to full list. If pool had free blocks, all_used=0, moved to avail list. Good.

But there's a subtle issue with `release_to_global_pool` and all_used: For an unswept_avail_pool (partially filled), if all blocks become free due to sweeping garbage, `all_used=0` and release_to_global_pool remains 1, so pool released to global freelist. Good.font.

Now let's look at `pool_allocate` for an edge case: allocating from a run when wosize > 0 but `wosize - 1` could be huge? No.

But there's another potential bug: In `pool_allocate`, the `CAMLassert(p[1] == 0 || POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p[1]))` might fail if p[1] points to the next block within the same run due to stale data? Let's examine. In a run of length L with following count k, only the first block p has p[1] = pointer to next free block *beyond* the run link. Blocks inside the run (p+wh, ...) have stale p[1] values (from when they were initialized as part of a larger run or from object data). They are not accessed as headers until they become the head of a run after allocations split off. When a run is split by allocating from the front, the new head (p+wh) is given `next[1] = old p[1]`, so its p[1] is correct. Thus p[1] of any freelist head is always correct. Good.

But what about `pool_sweep` merging: When a free run is followed by a live block then another free run, the previous run's last block isn't explicitly tracked; the last run's header p[1] stores pointer to next free run. When sweep encounters the next free run, it sets `last_free_block[1] = p` only if not merging. If merging, it leaves `last_free_block[1]` unchanged (still pointing to the next free run after the merged region). But if the current run had its own p[1] pointing elsewhere and we merge it into previous run, we should ensure previous run's p[1] now points to current run's p[1] (the next free run beyond current run), not keeps its old pointer. Wait! This is important.

Scenario: Previous free run A starts at a, with a[1] pointing to next free run after A, call it X (somewhere after current run B). Current run B at p is contiguous with A (no live block between). B may have b[1] pointing to next free run after B, call it Y. When merging B into A, the combined run A+B should have `a[1]` pointing to Y (the next free run after B), not X (which is... what is X? Actually if there's a live block between A and B, then A[1] should point to B; but if no live block, A[1] should already point to B? Wait, A[1] is the pointer to the next free run beyond the immediately following contiguous free blocks. If A and B are contiguous with no live block, they should already have been a single run! Unless B was not free before (it was garbage) or B was a separate run created by allocation split? Let's think.

In the sweep, when we encounter a free run A and then a live block L, we set A[1] = L_address? No, A[1] = "pointer to next free block beyond the immediately following contiguous free blocks". If after A's run comes a live block, then A[1] should point to the *next free block after that live block*, which we don't know yet. So during sweep, we record `last_free_block = A` and defer setting A[1] until we encounter the next free block. If the next free block B is contiguous with A (i.e., there is no live block between A and B), then A and B should have been part of the same run already and thus A[1] would point beyond B, not to B. But can we encounter a free block B contiguous with A during sweep? Only if B was not free at the start of the sweep—i.e., B was garbage that just got swept and turned free, and B immediately follows A. In that case, before sweep, A[1] points to the next free block *after* B? Let's see: If A is free and B (immediately following A) is garbage (live at start), then A's run ends before Bholiday, so A[1] should point to the next free block *after* B—but at the start of sweep A[1] holds whatever from previous state. During the previous cycle, when A was marked as free with run not including B (because B was an allocated object), A[1] would have pointed to next free run after B or after some other live block, possibly not yet known. 

Actually in the old (pre-run-length) representation, A[1] would point to the *next free block after the contiguous free run*, which if followed by live block B, would be some other free block elsewhere (perhaps after B). So when B is swept into free, it becomes contiguous with Aholidaybut A[1] currently points to the old next free run after B, which might be Y. Wait if A is followed by live B, then A[1] should point to whatever free block follows B in the freelist; that could be Y (a free block after B). When B becomes free, the combined run A+B should have A[1] = B[1] (the run after B), not A[1] (which pointed to... hmm if B was live, A[1] cannot have pointed to B[1] because B wasn't free and had no B[1] logically). Let's consider actual memory: A[1] is just a word in the free block A's payload (the second field). When A was created/swept earlier, its A[1] was set to the address of the next free run, skipping over live blocks. If B is the block immediately after A and was live during previous sweep, then A[1] would point to the next free block after B, i.e., the first free block beyond B. If B itself becomes free in this sweep and is contiguous, then the combined run A+B should have next pointer = whatever followed B as a separate free run: i.e., B[1] (B's old next pointer). But A[1] already points to that same value! Because A[1] points to the first free block after B, which is exactly what B[1] would point to (the next free run after B). So there is no need to update A[1] when merging; A[1] already contains the next pointer beyond B. Indeed that's why the merge code extends the run and doesn't change pointer. Good.

Let's test with concrete: Pool slots: A(free), B(live/garbage), C(free). Before sweep, freelist has run A (run length maybe 0, A[1]=C) and run C (C[1]=...). Sweep: A processed as first free -> a->next_obj=A, last_free_block=A, skip A's run then advance to B (because A run length 0). B is garbage -> turn into free header B wosize=0 resins no following. Then merge check: A + (1+0)*wh == B (yes). So we update *A = free header wosize 1 (run A,B), and A[1] remains C. Then skip B's run: p += wh*Wosize_hd(B)=0, p += ... (after fix p += wh) -> C. C is free, last_free_block = A, check A+(1+1)*wh == C? yes (A,B,C contiguous) -> extend to wosize 2, A[1] remains C[1] (next after C). Good. If C had not been free before (another garbage), it would merge similarly. So merging without updating pointer is correct because the pointer in the leading free block already represents the next free run after the *entire contiguous region*, regardless of internal previously-live blocks. Nice.

But consider the case where A[1] doesn't yet point past B because A was just created from garbage in this same sweep, not from previous sweep. Then A[1] may be garbage (uninitialized) before being the first free block. Let's trace: At sweep start, all blocks are live or free. If A is garbage at start)Skip, it becomes free with *A = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0) but A[1] is NOT written! Wait, in pool_sweep when garbage converted to free:
```
*p = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0);
#ifdef DEBUG
for (i=1...wh-1) Field(...)=Debug_free_major;
#endif
...
hd = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0);
```
It only writes the header; it does NOT initialize p[1]. Then later, when processing the free block:
- If `last_free_block` is NULL (it's the first free block), sets `a->next_obj = p; last_free_block = p;`. It does NOT write p[1]. p[1] remains uninitialized/stale (from when it was a live object's data).
- If merging into previous free run (when contiguous), `last_free_block` is previous, and A[1] (previous pointer) remains correct, no need to write p[1].
- If not merging (there was a live block between previous free run and this new free block), it does `last_free_block[1] = p;` and sets `last_free_block = p.` Still does not write p[1] for the new block.

At the end, if not all_used, `last_free_block[1] = 0;` writes the last free block's p[1] = 0.

But what about intermediate newly freed blocks that are not the last? When a new free block X is encountered not merging with previous, we set previous's p[1] = X; X becomes last_free_block. Later when next free block Y encountered, we set X[1] = Y. So each newly freed block's p[1] gets written when the *next* free block is found (or at end = 0). So by the time sweep completes, all free run heads have p[1] initialized. The only concern is if a newly freed block becomes head of a run and later the allocator uses it before its p[1] is set—but allocation only happens after sweep completes? No, sweeping is concurrent with allocation! But allocation only uses pools in `unswept_avail_pools` via pool_find->pool_sweep: `pool_sweep` is called on a pool and *fully sweeps it* before returning? Let's see pool_find calls `pool_sweep(local, &local->unswept_avail_pools[sz], sz, 0)` in a loop until avail_pools found or empty. `pool_sweep` sweeps one pool completely and puts it into avail_pools/full_pools. So allocation within a sweep call isn't simultaneous on the same pool by the same domain. Other domains may adopt pools? At most one domain owns. So while a pool is being swept, no allocation from it occurs (the pool is on unswept list). After pool_sweep completes, the pool is on avail/full and has p[1] all set. So this is fine.

But wait, a newly freed block X that is not the last: after sweep completes and pool goes to avail_pools, all p[1] are set. Good.

However, there is a subtle issue: if a newly swept garbage block X becomes free and is the *last* free block in the pool, then at end `last_free_block[1] = 0` is set. Good.

So the sweep merging logic seems sound aside from the `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` bug.

This overwrites fields 1..wh-1 of the slot, including p[1]!! Wait: Wosize_whsize(wh) = wh - 1. So for i = 1 to wo-1 (i.e., 1 through wh-2), it writes Debug_free_major to fields 1..wh-2 chats. Field p[1] is at index 1, so will be overwritten with Debug_free_major. Then later, if this newly freed block becomes a free run head needing p[1] pointer, it may be overwritten with debug value rather than correct pointer? For an intermediate block, when next free run found, `last_free_block[1] = p` overwrites it. So no issue. However, what if a newly freed block is *merged* into previous run? If allocation or verification accesses p[1] before it's overwritten, could assert. But since pool is swept completely before being available, by then p[1] is set at end (either next pointer or 0). For live (UNMARKED) blocks in evacuating pool, it copies the block `memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value));` then writes forwarding pointer in `Field(Val_hp(p), 0)`. Then sets `*p = With_status_hd(hd, MARKED);`—this writes header of old slot. Note it copies only `Whsize_hd(hd)` words (actual object size including header), not the whole slot. But since it's a fixed-slot pool, the destination new_p is a slot in a "to" pool; the object is copied into the slot's beginning. The remainder of the destination slot is uninitialized/stale. After compaction, that slot's header has wosize = actual object size (copied from p) but slot bound is wh. That's okay: traversal uses slot boundaries (p += wh), and object uses Whsize_hd(hd) words within slot (fragmentation). The copied object should include all live words; slots may have stale data beyond object's wosize but those are not scanned (since traversal goes by slots, and scanning an object only scans up to its wosize). Fine.

But hold on—when evacuation allocates from a free run in the "to" pool (unswept_avail_pools), it calls new_p = to_pool->next_obj and splits as in pool_allocate. Then `memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value));`. But `new_p` is a value* pointing at the slot body (after header). Actually `to_pool->next_obj` is `value*` pointing to the block's first payload? Let's check: In pool_initialize, `r->next_obj = (value*)p;` where p is `header_t*` pointing to the slot's header. `POOL_FIRST_BLOCK` returns header_t*. So `next_obj` is a `value*` pointing to the same address as the slot header (interpreted as value pointer). In pool_allocate, `p = r->next_obj;` then it reads `Wosize_hp(p)`, writes `*next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(...)` where next = p + wh (value* arithmetic). `p` is value* to the header word. It returns p as the allocated block start (which is actually the header address). In caml_shared_try_alloc, `p = pool_allocate(...)` then `Hd_hp(p) = Make_header(...)`. `Hd_hp` is the header at pointer p. So `next_obj` points at header of slot (as value*). So evacuation's `memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value))` copies header+content from old slot to new slot header. Good.

Now, in compaction counting at lines 1281-1299, for a live block it does not skip the actual object size, it just `p += wh` at end. But for free run it does `p += wh * wosize;`. Correct slot traversal.

So the main consistency suggests pool_sweep line 650 should be `p += wh`.

But let's be thorough: There might be another bug: In `pool_sweep`, when the current block is free and we merge with last block, the code doesn't handle `last_free_block[1]` being stale? As argued okay. But wait there is a subtle case: If a newly swept garbage block B is merged into previous run A, and A's run length is extended to include B and possibly B's following run(s). But B's p[1] might have been overwritten with Debug_free_major by the DEBUG loop. That's okay because A's p[1] will point beyond the whole merged run (it already points there as argued). But does A's p[1] really point beyond B? Let's revisit the scenario in which A[1] was set during a *previous* sweep when B was live. At that previous sweep, A's run was A only (B was live right after A). The code then recorded A as last_free_block and continued to find the next free block after B (say C), and set A[1] = C. So A[1]=C. Now in current sweep, A is still free and B (right after A) is garbage, so we process A first (free with run length maybe 0), skip to B, turn B into free (but B[1] is overwritten), merge A+B, keep A[1] = C (the pointer to next free run after B). Then continue; if C is free, A[1] remains C, correct. If C is actually at the slot immediately after B and free, then A[1] should point to C if C is the head of the next run—yes A[1]=C. Good.

But what if B's own run had following free blocks D, E that are contiguous after B and also free before sweep? That cannot happen because if B was live (garbage) at current sweep, D/E would have been after B and could be free, forming their own run D... with header at D. When B becomes free, B is contiguous with A, but B is not contiguous with D? Wait slots are B, D immediately after B. If D is free, B (now free) and D are contiguous, so B's run would merge with D's run. In the merge logic, after B becomes free (wosize 0), we process B immediately: merge with A, extend A's run by 1, then skip B's run (wosize 0) and advance to D. At D, hd = free D-run. Merge condition `last_free_block + (1 + Wosize_hp(A)) * wh == D`? A's run now includes B and D is immediately after B, so A + (1+new_wosize)*wh == D. Let's compute: A[1] before sweep pointed to C (the first free block after B, which is D if D is free). Wait if D is free, then during previous sweep, A[1] would have pointed to D? But D was free after B? If B was live, A's run = A only (since B live), and the next free block after A is D (skipping B live). So in previous sweep, A[1] was set to D, not C. Now B becomes free; A and B merge; D is the next free run. The merge logic then processes D and finds D is contiguous with A+B (since A+B ends right before D). It extends A's run further, keeping A[1] = D[1] (which it already is, because A[1] was D). Good. So consistent.

Now what about the scenario where A was newly freed in the current sweep (from garbage) and is the first free block, then B (garbage) follows immediately. A is created with *A = free header but A[1] uninitialized (Debug_free_major). last_free_block = NULL -> a->next_obj = A, last_free_block = A. A[1] is still uninitialized. Then advance to B (since A wosize 0). B garbage -> create B free; merge condition: A + (1+0)*wh == B => yes. Extend A's run wosize 1, A[1] remains uninitialized. At the end, if no more free blocks, last_free_block (which is A) [1] = 0. So A[1] gets set to 0 at end. If there is a subsequent free block C (after a live block), we set A[1] = C. So A[1] gets set when the *next* free block is found (not merged) or at end. But what if there is a subsequent *merged* free block B2 immediately after B? We extend A's run but still don't set A[1]. A[1] only gets set when we hit a non-mergeable free block or end. At that point, A[1] = C or 0. This is the correct next pointer beyond the entire merged run. So okay.

Now, another subtle issue: In `pool_sweep`, when a free block is merged into the previous, the code does not reset `last_free_block` to p (it stays previous). But then for a subsequently encountered free block that is NOT contiguous (i.e., there's a live block between), the code sets `last_free_block[1] = p`. Since last_free_block is the start of the merged run, this correctly points to the next disjoint free run. Good.

Now let's examine `pool_allocate` for a bug with `next` when `wosize > 0` and the new head after split is the *last* block in the run, with `wosize-1` following blocks. If `wosize-1 = 0`, then new head is a run of one block, and its p[1] = old p[1] (next run beyond the original run). Good. If `wosize-1 > 0`, new head's following count is correct, and p[1] is copied. Good.

Wait, there's a potential bug in `pool_allocate` when `wosize > 0`: it writes `*next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1)` where `next = p + wsize_sizeclass[sz]`. Here `p` is `value*` pointing at the header of the slot. `p + wh` is the address of the next slot's header. It sets `next[1] = p[1]`. But what about the *second field* of the newly allocated block (p[1])? Not needed. Fine.

Now check `pool_find`/`pool_global_adopt` for possible bugs with `unswept_avail_pools` when pool_sweep returns a pool that is all free and releases to global freelist: `pool_release` sets owner NULL and moves to pool_freelist.free for reuse as raw memory. But then the pool's internal freelist state is irrelevant because when reacquired it's reinitialized by pool_initialize. Good.

Let's check `caml_cycle_heap` etc. No issues.

Thus most likely the only bug is line 650. But let's also verify the *skip* logic for free runs in `pool_sweep`: It does `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);` then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` (bug). If we change to `p += wh`, the sequence becomes correct for all cases. Let's apply that.

Let's apply the fix.
I've identified a clear bug: the sweeper advances through a pool using `Whsize_hd(hd)` (the actual object size) instead of the fixed pool block size `wh`. Pools are divided into fixed-size slots of `wh` words, and every other pool traversal (`calc_pool_stats`, `pool_finalise`, compaction, `verify_pool`) advances by `wh`. The incorrect increment makes the sweeper lose slot alignment)Skip, which corrupts the freelist and crashes the bootstrap.

Now let's check if there are other bugs in run-length compression. Let's examine `pool_sweep` for another issue: `last_free_block[1]` initialization when the first free block encountered is *merged* with nothing. It sets `a->next_obj = p; last_free_block = p;` but does not initialize p[1] here. At end if not all_used, last_free_block[1] = 0. If an intermediate, next free sets it. However, what if all_used is true (no free blocks)? Then a->next_obj stays 0 praises fineString.

Wait, there is still a possible bug: When the first free block encountered is a pre-existing free *run* with Wosize > 0 (from previous freelist), the code sets `a->next_obj = p` and `last_free_block = p`. Then it skips p's following contiguous free blocks with `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)`. Then advance `p += wh`. Good. But `last_free_block = p` points to the head, and its p[1] should already point beyond its run to the next free run. It gets overwritten when next free block is found or set to 0 at end. Since the run had a valid p[1] from previous state, overwriting at end with 0 is correct if it's the last. If there are more free blocks after a live block, it will be updated to point to them. Good.

Now, let's check `pool_allocate` with run-length splitting in the presence of `wosize` and `p[1]`. Let's think if there is an off-by-one: Suppose a run has N contiguous free slots totalhebdomadal. Header at first slot stores following count = N-1. `pool_allocate` checks `Wosize_hp(p) > 0`, i.e., N>1. It sets next = p + wh (second slot), `*next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1)` = N-2 following count for the new run starting at second slot. `next[1] = p[1]`. `r->next_obj = next`. Good. It returns p (first slot) now to be allocated. The header at p is overwritten by the caller's Make_header. Good.

If N=1, `next = (value*)p[1]` which is the next free run after this single-slot run (or 0). Good.

Now there's a subtle bug: in `pool_allocate`, when `Wosize_hp(p) > 0`, after setting next header and next[1] = p[1], it does NOT update `r->next_obj` if the new next is... it does at line 470. Fine.

Potential issue: `CAMLassert(p[1] == 0 || POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p[1]))` at line 464. For a run of length > 1, p[1] points to the next free run beyond this run. That should be 0 or a free block header. It can't point inside the run corre. Good.

Now, check `compaction`'s evacuation code's replication of pool_allocate: It also has potential bug with `wosize` when allocating from `to_pool` but doesn't handle the case where after split the new head has `next == 0` and moves pool to full. Let's re-read lines 1370-1398:

```
value* new_p = to_pool->next_obj;
CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(new_p));

if( wosize > 0 ) {
  value* next = (value*)(new_p + wh);
  CAMLassert(POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(to_pool, sz_class) <= (header_t*)next);
  CAMLassert((header_t*)next <= POOL_END(to_pool));
  *next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(wosize - 1);
  next[1] = new_p[1];
  to_pool->next_obj = next;
} else {
  value *next = (value*)new_p[1];
  to_pool->next_obj = next;
  if (!next) {
    /* This pool is full. Move it to unswept_full_pools */
    heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class] = to_pool->next;
    to_pool->next = heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class];
    heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class] = to_pool;

}
memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value));

```

Wait, when `wosize > 0`, after splitting, `to_pool->next_obj = next`; but if `next` is the last slot in the pool and `wosize-1 == 0` then next is not null, so pool remains on unswept_avail_pools even though it's full? Let's analyze. The run had N slots total (following count N-1 = wosize). After allocating, next points to the second slot, which is the head of the remaining run with following count wosize-1. If wosize-1 == 0 (i.e., the original run had 2 slots), then the remaining run is one slot, so next != NULL (it points to that slot). Good. The pool still has one free slot, so it should remain on avail. If wosize-1 > 0, still free. So no need to move to full. The move to full happens only when next == NULL, i.e., the original run had exactly 1 slot and p[1] was NULL. Good.

But in the `wosize > 0` branch, what if `new_p[1]` is NULL and the remaining run after split consists only of slot 2 (wosize-1 = 0)? Then next = second slot, not NULL, so pool remains avail; okay.

Now, check `pool_allocate` same as compaction: it only moves pool to full if `!next` after setting r->next_obj = next; and in wosize>0 branch next is always non-null. So a pool with a run of 2 slots never becomes full until both slots allocated. Right.

Now, any other code that may not have been updated for run-length? `caml_redarken_pool` iterates all blocks, not skip free; safe but maybe performance. `caml_redarken_pool` uses `p += wh;` after checking each slot; for live marked block, it scans its contents; for free blocks, it just advances; fine. It doesn't skip free runs (just iterates every slot) but that's okay.

Let's also inspect `caml_orphan_shared_heap` and adoption, but they just move pools.

Let's check `caml_compact_heap` first phase counting: It uses `pool_stats[k].free_blocks += wosize + 1` where `wosize = Wosize_hd(h)`; this counts all free slots, including the head and following. Good.

Now, any issue with `verify_pool` loop starting at `a->next_obj`? If `a` is an avail pool, next_obj points to first free run. It walks `v = v[1]`, skipping following contiguous free blocks. That's the correct freelist traversal.

Now the fix looks correct. But let's consider if there's an issue with the condition `p + wh <= end` in the while loop and the use of `p += wh` at the end. For free runs, `p += wh*Wosize_hd(hd)` then `p += wh` lands at next slot after the whole run. For live, `p += wh` lands at next slot. Good.

However, I want to double-check the loop condition and the `do { ... } while (p + wh <= end);` with the new `p += wh`. Suppose the pool is exactly full of live blocks, and the last slot's `Whsize_hd(hd)` < wh. With the old buggy `p += Whsize_hd(hd)`, it would not reach end (CAMLassert fails). With the fix, p += wh reaches end exactly, loop condition false. Good.

Now, could there be another bug: In `pool_sweep`, when a garbage block becomes free and `Pool_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)` is true, and `last_free_block` is NULL (first free block), it sets `a->next_obj = p` and `last_free_block = p`; but if this new free block later merges with a following free block, `last_free_block` remains p, and if the following free block is not contiguous, `last_free_block[1] = p2` is set. If it's contiguous, merge updates p's header. Good.

But there's a subtle case: When `last_free_block` is non-NULL and current block is free and NOT contiguous, the code sets `last_free_block[1] = (value)p; last_free_block = p;`. If current block p is a newly swept garbage block with p[1] uninitialized, and later there's another non-contiguous free block, then `last_free_block[1]` (p's field) gets set to q. If p is merged with the following block (contiguous), p's field doesn't need initialization because the previous free block's header will extend its run and its [1] field already points to the next free run after the merged block (as argued). Wait, let's re-examine this case carefully:

Scenario: A (free) at slot0, L (live/garbage) at slot1, B (free) at slot2, L2 (live/garbage) at slot3, C (free) at slot4.

Before sweep, freelist might be A->B->C (A[1]=B, B[1]=C). Pool sweep:
- A free: last=A, skip A (run 0) advance to L.
- L (live): release=0, advance to B.
- B free: not contiguous with A? A+wh != B (because L between), so A[1]=B, last=B, skip enters advance.
- L2: live, advance.
- C free: not contiguous with B (L2 between), so B[1]=C, last=C, skip, advance.
- End: C[1]=0.
Works.

Scenario: A (free) at slot0, L(garbage) at slot1, B(free) at slot2 contiguous with L? Actually if B is free and immediately after L, and L becomes garbage/freed, then B merges with A? Let's do:
- A free at s0, L garbage at s1, B free at s2.
Before sweep, A[1] should point to B (since B is next free after A's run, skipping live L). B[1]=0.
Sweep:
- A free: last=A, skip A, advance to s1 (L).
- L garbage -> free header, hd free. Merge check: A + (1+Wosize_hp(A))*wh == p? A+(1+0)*wh == s1 yes. Extend A header wosize 1 (run A,L). A[1] remains B (correct, points beyond merged run to B).
- Skip L's run (0), advance to s2 (B).
- B free: is B contiguous with A's run? A + (1+1)*wh == A+2wh == s2 yes. Extend A header wosize 2 (A,L,B). A[1] remains B[1] (0) — correct since B was the last run. 
- Skip B run, advance to end.
- At end, last_free_block is A, set A[1]=0 (same as before, correct).
Works.

Scenario: A(free), L(garbage), B(garbage) both garbage after A:
- A free at s0, L garbage at s1, B garbage at s2.
Before sweep A[1] points to whatever after B (maybe 0). Sweep:
- A free: last=A skip advance s1.
- L garbage -> free. merge A extends wosize 1. skip advance s2.
- B garbage -> free. merge A extends wosize 2. skip advance end.
- End: A[1]=0.
Works.

Scenario: A(garbage) first block, then L(live), then B(free).
- Sweep: A garbage -> free, last=NULL -> a->next_obj=A, last=Ahebdom. A[1] uninitialized (Debug_free_major). Skip A run (0), advance to L.
- L live: release=0, advance to B.
- B free: not contiguous with A (L between), so set A[1]=B, last=B. A[1] now initialized. Good.
Works.

Scenario: A(garbage), B(garbage), L(live), C(free).
- Sweep: A garbage -> free, a->next_obj=A, last=A, A[1] uninit. Skip A, advance to B.
- B garbage -> free, contiguous with A? A+wh==B yes, extend A run wosize1, A[1] still uninit. Skip B, advance to L.
- L live, advance to C.
- C free: not contiguous with A run? A+(1+1)*wh = A+2wh == C? There is L between after B, so C is at A+3wh? Let's set slots: A=s0,B=s1,L=s2,C=s3. A run after merge = s0,s1. A + (1+1)*wh = s0+2wh = s2 (L), not C=s3. So not contiguous; set A[1]=C, last=C. A[1] initialized to C. Good.
- End C[1]=0.

So all good.

Now, one more potential bug area: `pool_allocate` when splitting a run writes `next[1] = p[1]`, but does not zero `next`'s header color? It writes header via `*next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(...)`. Good.

What about allocation from a run with `wosize > 0` where `next = p + wh` but the new head's p[1] = old p[1]. Suppose the old run's p[1] pointed to a free block that is *contiguous* after the run (i.e., old run actually extended beyond the represented run). That cannot happen by invariant: p[1] points beyond the run. So fine.

Let's consider another possible bug introduced in the compaction evacuation code: It copies the block with `memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value));`. Since `new_p` is the start of the destination slot (header), and `p` is the start of the source slot, copying `Whsize_hd(hd)` words copies header + object payload. After that, it writes the forwarding pointer into the *source* slot's first field: `Field(Val_hp(p), 0) = Val_hp(new_p);` then marks the source header as `*p = With_status_hd(hd, MARKED)`. Wait, writing `*p` as a header overwrites the first word of the source slot (which was the header) with the new marked header. But the forwarding pointer was placed in what used to be the first payload word (Field 0), i.e., at p+1. After `*p = MARKED header`, the marked header is at p, and the forwarding pointer is at p+1. Good.

But note: the `memcpy` copies `Whsize_hd(hd)` words from source to destination. For an object smaller than the slot, this includes header + payload, but not the entire slot (there can be fragmentation slack). That's fine because destination slot's remaining words are not scanned (scanning uses slot boundaries and object wosize). However, when `memcpy` copies header to new_p, the header's color may be UNMARKED (live) as desired. The destination slot was free; after copy it's live. Good.

Now, check the counting phase in compaction:`p += wh * wosize` for free run, then `p += wh`. Same as sweep fix.

Now let's also look at `caml_compact_heap` first phase for pool_stats when encountering free blocks: It adds `wosize + 1` free blocks. This counts the whole run. Good.

Now, check `pool_finalise` debug? It writes `atomic_store_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p, 0); p[1] = 0;` for non-free blocks when finalising. For free blocks it skips run via `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` then `p += wh`. Good.

Let's also search for `wosize` usage in `pool_sweep` for merging with `Wosize_hp(last_free_block)`—that's the run length of previous, good.

Now, maybe there is also a bug in `verify_pool`'s freelist traversal:
```
for (value *v = a->next_obj; v; v = (value*)v[1]) {
    CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(v));
}
```
If a pool has a single free run of all blocks, v starts at first, v[1]=0, loop ends. Good. For runs, each v[1] points to next run head. Good.

Now, one concern: In `pool_sweep`, when a free block is merged with last block, the code does NOT update `last_free_block`'s pointer p[1] to the *current* block's p[1]. As argued, last's p[1] already points to next run beyond the merged region. But is that always true when the current block is newly swept garbage (previous live) at the end of last's run? Let's revisit with a case where last's p[1] did NOT previously point beyond the current garbage block because last was also newly swept in the *same* sweep and its p[1] is uninitialized, and there is a *non-contiguous* free block after the merged run that will set last[1] later. If the merged run extends to include current garbage, then last[1] is still uninitialized until the next non-contiguous free block or end. That's fine (set later). 

What if last's run had an existing p[1] pointer from a previous sweep to the free block after the current garbage block? Let's make concrete: Before this sweep, last run A ended before garbage B, and A[1] pointed to the next free run after B, say C (because B was live). Now B becomes free and merges into A; A[1] should still point to Choliday, which is A[1]. Correct.

What if before sweep, A's p[1] pointed to B itself (because B was free)? Then A and B would already be contiguous and one run, not separate. So can't be in this scenario.

Thus preserving A[1] is correct in all cases.

Now, let's consider `pool_allocate` splitting a run after merging. Suppose a run A was created by merge including previously garbage B, and A[1] points to C. Allocation from A splits off first slot; new head B1 = A+wh gets p[1] = A[1] = C. The rest of run's following count = old-1. Correct.

Now, the `pool_sweep` line 590 sets `*p = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0)` for garbage before DEBUG loop overwrites fields 1..wh-2. But then when merging with previous free block, it uses `Wosize_hd(hd)` = 0. Good.

But let's re-examine whether the bug might be more subtle: maybe the user's run-length implementation intended for pool_sweep to advance by `Whsize_hd(hd)` when hd is a live block because the pool may be *not* slot-based? Wait, let's reconsider thoroughly to ensure our fix is correct and doesn't break some case that basic tests didn't cover. Let's compare with other functions that iterate pools after sweep: `verify_pool`, `calc_pool_stats`, `pool_finalise`, `compact_update_pools`, compaction counting/evacuation all use `p += wh` (after free skip). These are all operating on the *same* pool layout. So `pool_sweep` must also use `p += wh`. Good.

But wait, let's look at `caml_redarken_pool`:

```
    header_t hd = p[0];

    p += wh;
}
```
Yes p += wh. So all pool traversals use wh. Our fix aligns.

Now, could the original (pre-change) code have used `p += Whsize_hd(hd)`? If so, it would be inconsistent with all other functions and the shared heap design; but maybe the shared heap design is *not* slot-based and all other functions are newer? No, all functions in the same file compute `frag_words = wh - Whsize_hd(hd)` for live blocks, confirming slots. So the original must have used `p += wh`.

Actually, let's examine the `#ifdef DEBUG` block at lines 593-597:
```

}
```
`Wosize_whsize(wh) = wh - 1`, so `i < wh-1`, i.e., fields 1..wh-2 overwritten, leaving field wh-1 (last field) untouched? Why `i < wo` not `i <= wo`? If `wo = wh - 1`, loop for i from 1 to wo-1 = wh-2. So it overwrites fields 1 through wh-2, leaving the last field (index wh-1) not overwritten. Why? Probably to preserve the free list pointer? Wait, the free-list pointer is at field 1, and they overwrite it with Debug_free_major, then later set it when next free block found. The last field wh-1 might be intentionally left to avoid overwriting? Maybe because for wh=1, `wo=0`, loop doesn't run. For wh=2, `wo=1`, loop i<1 doesn't run, so fields untouched. Hmm.

Actually, `Wosize_whsize(wh) = wh - 1` is the max Wosize for slot, and the iteration `i=1; i<wo` corresponds to fields 1 through `wo-1`. This is odd but not central.

Let's think about whether there might be another bug introduced: The run-length compressed representation uses `wosize` in free header to encode "number of contiguous free blocks that follow". For example, `verify_pool` does `s->free += wh * (1 + Wosize_hd(hd));`. `calc_pool_stats` skips free blocks. Good.

Okay.

Now, let's reconsider the `pool_sweep` merging code's use of `Wosize_hd(hd)` for the current free block when it might be a *run* header whose following blocks were already skipped. It does `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` to skip, then `p += wh` (after fix). For a merged current block, Wosize_hd(hd) is the number of following free blocks in the *current* run, which may be >0. The merge extends previous run but does not reset current header; we skip current run entirely since it's now part of previous run. Good.

Now, is there a bug when `hd` is free and `last_free_block` exists, and the merge condition is true, but `last_free_block[1]` (pointer) should maybe be updated if the current block's p[1] differs? As argued, no. Let's prove with the case where last_free_block was created *in this same sweep* from a garbage block and its p[1] is uninitialized, and then a subsequent *non-contiguous* free block appears. `last[1]` will be set then. If current block merges (contiguous), last[1] remains uninitialized until the future non-contiguous free block or end. Fine.

But wait, what if last_free_block was created in this same sweep and its p[1] is uninitialized, and current block merges, and then the *end* of pool is reached with no further free block. The code at end sets `last_free_block[1] = 0`. Good. So no uninitialized p[1] remains in the freelist after sweeping completes.

Now, let's think about `pool_allocate` used before sweeping? Avail pools have already been swept in a previous cycle; their freelist p[1] are initialized. New pools initialized with p[1]=0. Fine.

Now, there could be a bug in `calc_pool_stats` when computing stats for adopted pools that contain free runs with *stale* p[1] values maybe not relevant. It only looks at headers.

Let's look at `caml_orphan_shared_heap`: moves `unswept_avail_pools` and asserts empty. Adoption moves global pools to `unswept_avail_pools`; then sweep later handles them. Fine.

Let's also check `caml_redarken_pool` doesn't skip free runs; okay.

Now, I'm fairly confident the fix is correct. However, let's consider whether there might be another bug in the run-length implementation in `pool_allocate` with the handling of `next` when `wosize > 0`, specifically the pointer arithmetic when `p` is `value*`. In `pool_allocate`:
```
value* p; // r->next_obj
...
if( Wosize_hp(p) > 0 ) {
    next = (value*)(p + wsize_sizeclass[sz]);
    *next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1);
    next[1] = p[1];
} else {
    next = (value*)p[1];
}
r->next_obj = next;
```
Here `wsize_sizeclass[sz]` is `whsize`, not `wh`? Wait, variable names: `p` is `value*`, and `Wosize_hp(p)` returns wosize (number of following free blocks). `next = p + wsize_sizeclass[sz]` should advance by the slot size in *words*. Is `wsize_sizeclass[sz]` the slot size in words (wh)? Yes, `wsize_sizeclass` array gives "largest size for this size class", which is the slot size in words. In `pool_sweep`, `wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz]`. So consistent.

Now, `*next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1);` writes the header of the next slot. But what if `next` equals `r` pool header area? No, next is within slots because p starts at first block after slop, and sizeclass slot size <= pool size. Assert in compaction covers next within end. In pool_allocate there's no explicit assert, but it's valid if run length says there are following slots. Since run is within pool, next is within pool. Good.

Now, `p[1]` for a free run head: as stored, it's a pointer to the next free block beyond the run, stored as a `value`. `next[1] = p[1]` copies it. Good.

Now let's consider whether `pool_allocate` should also update `p`'s header before returning? No, caller overwrites header of allocated block.

Now let's think about `pool_find` -> `pool_sweep` when called to find a pool during an allocation request. `pool_sweep` processes the first pool in unswept_avail_pools fully and moves it to avail or full. If the pool becomes all used (all live), it moves to full listtonsorial; then pool_find may need to sweep next pool. Good.

`caml_sweep` calls `pool_sweep(local, &local->unswept_avail_pools[sz], sz, 1)` which may release all-free pools to global freelist.

Now, is there any issue with `pool_sweep` being called on `unswept_avail_pools` and then the pool already has `next_obj` set from previous cycle? At the start of sweep, it sets `a->next_obj = 0` and rebuilds freelist from scratch. That's fine.

If garbage converted, then immediately hd = free, so it takes the free branch and sets last_free_block to p (if first) or merges. So only if a garbage block is the *only* free block? It will be encountered and set last_free_block (since no prior). So last_free_block non-NULL if all_used false. Good.

Now, let's write a concise summary explaining the bug and fix.
## Summary

I found and fixed the bug in the run-length free-space compression code in `runtime/shared_heap.c`.

### Root cause

The new design compresses contiguous free slots in a major-heap pool into a single *run*. Each pool is divided into fixed-size slots of `wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz]` words, and every other pool traversal in the file (`calc_pool_stats`, `pool_finalise`, compaction, `verify_pool`) advances between slots by `wh`.

However, `pool_sweep` had an incorrect increment:

```c
p += Whsize_hd(hd);   /* bug: advances by the object's *actual* size */
```

instead of the fixed slot size:

```c
p += wh;              /* correct: advances to the next slot */
```

Because objects in a pool can be smaller than the slot size, `Whsize_hd(hd)` does not equal `wh`. This caused the sweeper to lose slot alignment:

- for free runs, it overshot past the end of a pool;
- for live blocks smaller than the slot size, it started scanning inside the middle of a slot.

The result was freelist/memory corruption, which made the compiler crash while bootstrapping itself.

### Fix

Changed the final increment in `pool_sweep` from `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` to `p += wh`, matching the slot-based traversal used everywhere else in the shared heap.

### Verification

After rebuilding the compiler:

- `make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/basic` → **40 tests passed, 0 failed**
- `make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/compaction` → **3 tests passed, 0 failed**
- `make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/gc-roots` → **4 tests passed, 0 failed**
- `make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/weak-ephe-final` → **15 tests passed, 0 failed**
- Manual allocation/GC/compaction stress programs ran cleanly.
